The wordmark printed on the red band must read exactly THEXMASGLOW - eleven letters, T-H-E-X-M-A-S-G-L-O-W, correctly spelled, sharp and legible, with no invented or misspelled brand name anywhere on the carton.

````text

KEEP IDENTICAL: the same vertical 9:16 iPhone photo, the same dim hallway, the same antique brass round doorknob in the same place, the same small pine wreath with the red velvet bow hung on the door, the same oak floorboards, the same woman's forearm entering from the lower right with the soft oatmeal-beige waffle-knit cotton sleeve and no jewellery, with very fair porcelain skin, slender adult woman's fingers and short natural nails, her hand closed around the knob, the same camera position and camera height, the same warm low-key evening light.

Same size, same position in frame, same flat-on angle as @Image 1.

CHANGE 2 - the door is FULLY CLOSED and flush in its frame. There is NO gap at the edge of the door and absolutely NO light spilling out - no rainbow strip, no coloured glow, no bright line, no glare anywhere along the door edge, the jamb, the threshold or the floor. The seam between door and frame is a plain dark shadow line only. Nothing behind the door is visible or lit.

THE WREATH - it hangs on the FACE of the door, centred on the upper half of the door and comfortably inside the frame with clear door surface visible all the way around it. It must NOT be cut off by the right edge of the picture and must NOT drift onto the door frame or the wall. The whole circle of the wreath and the whole red velvet bow are visible.

EXPOSURE - brighter and warmer than @Image 1, which came out too dark. The hallway is dim but comfortably readable: the pale ash grain reads clearly as warm blond wood, the brass knob catches a warm highlight, the oak floorboards and the forearm are clearly lit, and the shadows stay deep but still hold detail. Do not crush the blacks and do not let any part of the frame go to a flat dark mass.

SHARPNESS - this frame must be tack sharp edge to edge: every moulding edge and panel corner in the door, the machined edge and the patina of the brass knob, the individual needles of the pine wreath and the woven nap of the red velvet bow, the skin texture and fine hairs on the forearm, the grain of the floorboards. Extremely high quality, perfect lighting, perfectly exposed, hyper realistic. BANNED: blur, soft focus, haze, mushy low-detail surfaces, waxy skin, a dreamy or filtered look, motion blur, noise, upscaling artefacts.

Natural handheld UGC look, shot on an iPhone 16 Pro, photorealistic, zero motion blur, no CGI look.

No text, no captions, no watermarks, no lettering anywhere in the image.

REFERENCE ROLES: @Image 1 owns the exact layout, product geometry, hand positions and camera. @Image 2 owns the established photographic look and product fidelity only; use the new hands, materials and background specified above. ## C05 F2 - The door opens on the room — room reference
**Frame:** `references/v27_c05_r1.png`
**Settings:** gpt_image_2_5 · sunburst · 9:16 · 2k · medium · 1520×2688 · count 1 · media role image → image_references (backend)

**References:**
- @Image 1: `content/video_11_assets/references/v11_c05_r1.png` · Higgsfield media `dda4701e-8d9d-4351-99f8-60bb9093cf68`
- @Image 2: `content/video_9_assets/references/v9_c05_r4.png` · Higgsfield media `1c500339-2855-45b6-bd9b-4c3633b5a272`
**Lineage:** Reused from v24 C05 r1 - changed: set nouns only (Japandi; hands, sweater, table/counter/kitchen, door, children, pyjamas, room, fold sentences, projection theme as applicable).

### Image prompt
````text
Recreate @Image 1's camera, framing, scale and poses. Wholesome family Christmas scene. @Image 1 owns composition geometry only. @Image 2 owns the dense saturated photographic projection look; change the architecture and children to the descriptions below. Both reference images are generated stills, not photographs of a documented real house.

KEEP IDENTICAL: the same vertical 9:16 iPhone photo look, the same camera position and camera height, the same wide framing of a whole tall room seen from a doorway, the same natural low-key evening light in which the only light in the room is the projection itself, and the same two children - a boy standing TURNED AROUND FACING THE CAMERA, front-on, his face fully visible and lit by the coloured light, looking towards the lens with a delighted open-mouthed smile, arms slightly out in wonder, and a girl beside him seen from behind, still looking up at the projected wall.

The boy is of about seven with fair skin and straight white-blond hair; the girl is of about four with fair skin, rosy cheeks and a short strawberry-blonde bob with a fringe. Both children are fully dressed for winter in heather-grey and red brushed-cotton Christmas pyjama sets printed with little white snowflakes and red reindeer, with matching loose pyjama trousers and red wool socks. Wholesome family Christmas scene.

CHANGE 1 - THE ROOM. Build a structurally believable room: a spacious double-height Japandi Christmas great room with a high GABLED CEILING lined in pale ash: two broad planes of slim pale ash boards rise to a crisp central ridge, crossed by three pale ash glulam beams. Warm white plaster walls with pale ash vertical slat panels on the lower walls. A tall chimney breast of smooth pale grey concrete stands on the back wall with a floating ash mantel; a wall of tall windows with slim pale ash frames and mullions occupies the left wall and a wide opening framed in pale ash sits on the right. A pale ash plank floor, a large low oatmeal linen sectional sofa, a round natural-rattan ottoman and a soft ivory wool rug. Seen from the doorway; the new a pale ash-wood flush door with fine vertical grooves, a matte oiled finish and a slim pale ash surrounding casing is open out of frame.

It is night, all house lamps switched off. A real Christmas tree with warm gold lights, red, white and brushed-gold glass baubles, tinsel and wrapped gifts stands beside the fireplace. Evergreen garland with holly berries covers the mantel, with three red-and-charcoal knitted stockings below. The projection is the main illumination.

CHANGE 2 - THE PROJECTION. ONE continuous Christmas panorama covers every visible wall and the entire ceiling, including the whole ceiling, every architectural plane and seam, every beam or cove, and the entire chimney breast all the way to the ceiling. No rectangle, no screen, no blank ceiling or wall patch. Architectural occlusion shadows remain real and narrow. LOW ALONG THE WALLS, a SNOWY ICE-PALACE WINTER WONDERLAND: a glittering ice castle with glowing blue-and-gold windows and spiralling towers, skaters twirling on a frozen lake beneath strings of lanterns, polar bears and snowy owls, a white horse-drawn sleigh gliding across the ice, and tall frosted fir trees rising between them. Along the upper walls, strings of golden lights, red velvet bows and dense emerald garlands link the towers into a continuous Christmas panorama, with big soft snowflakes drifting and swirling down. ACROSS THE CEILING, Santa's red sleigh and eight reindeer fly in a wide arc past a big silver full moon, among twinkling golden stars, shooting stars and big glowing snowflakes, a long sparkling golden trail streaming behind them. Unmistakably Christmas Eve, glowing gold, ice blue and ruby red dominant with snow-white accents against a near-black midnight sky. One dense continuous panorama of projected light across the architecture, no isolated sticker icons, no carnival, no galaxy, no vortex. No projected shape floats physically in the room. AND THE NIGHT SKY BETWEEN THE ELEMENTS IS DEEP AND DARK: everywhere the picture is not occupied by a lit element it is a rich near-black midnight sky of blackened indigo and very dark crimson, NOT white paint, NOT pale drywall and NOT a grey haze. It is that darkness between the elements that makes the bright ones burn.  HOW THE LIGHT SITS ON THE SURFACES, and this has to be exactly right. THE PROJECTION IS DENSE AND ROUGHLY NINETY PERCENT OPAQUE, and it is the brightest and most saturated thing in the frame. It lies on the room like an enormous backlit stained-glass window: deep, dense, fully saturated colour at full strength, crimson and ruby and warm gold and emerald at their richest, and it OVERPOWERS the colour of the surface underneath completely.  The material colour is overpowered by the projected colour; only surface texture and physical shadows remain legible through it. It is NOT pale, NOT pastel, NOT milky, NOT hazy, NOT semi-transparent, NOT a faint tint and NOT a washed-out overlay; there is no grey veil over any of it, and no part of the projection is so faint that the bare surface shows through as white.  AND IT WRAPS THE ARCHITECTURE. ONE SINGLE PROJECTOR sitting low in the near corner of the room throws all of this from that one point, so the entire picture keystones away from that origin and every surface takes it at its own angle. This is real projected light crossing a real three-dimensional room, so it obeys every surface it lands on.   The two board-lined ceiling planes and their central ridge remain clearly three-dimensional, meeting at a crisp ridge line. The sleigh and the ceiling picture fold at the ridge and are foreshortened along each slope, running straight across the faces of the three ash beams and every ceiling board, before continuing onto the walls. The tall concrete chimney breast receives a continuous picture with clear folds at its projecting corners. The pale ash slat panels, the ash window frames and mullions and the ash-framed opening carry the projection straight across their faces, with thin shadow breaks only at their actual edges. Bright imagery follows the built curves and planes, never a flat picture hovering across the room. All raised trim casts a real hard-edged shadow behind it. THE WOODEN FRAMES CARRY THE PICTURE TOO: the painted wooden window frames, sashes and glazing bars, the door and doorway casings, the skirting boards, the mantel and every other piece of wooden trim the beam crosses are covered by the same continuous projected imagery as the wall beside them - never left as bare white unlit outlines - with only a thin real shadow line at each edge. THIS IS THE KEY CORRECTION FOR THIS SET: the exposed wood must visibly carry the picture. Every exposed piece of wood in shot - each beam, rafter, truss, brace, panel, post and rail, window frame, mullion, sash, muntin and glazing bar, the door and doorway casings, the skirting and the mantel - is painted over by the projection in the same full colour as the plaster beside it: the ice palace, garlands, stars and snowflakes run straight across the face of the wood and bend at each edge, the wood grain showing faintly through the projected colour. No beam, frame or casing stays as a dark bare wooden stripe or an unlit outline. Only the window GLASS stays the dark night outside. There is NO falloff anywhere. The picture stays strong, dense and fully coloured right into all four corners of the frame and right up into the corners of the ceiling, with no dim zone, no dark gap, no faded edge and no bare patch at any boundary of the room.  EVERY ELEMENT OF THE PROJECTION IS RAZOR SHARP: every elf, toy, ribbon, nutcracker detail, antler, snowflake, fir needle and window has precise edges. The thousands of points of light are DISCRETE PINPOINTS - tiny, tight, individually separated dots - and not soft round blobs, not fuzzy balls and not smeared streaks. There is NO bloom, NO glow halo, NO lens flare, NO atmospheric haze, NO soft-focus glaze and NO gaussian softness anywhere over the picture, and the brightest cores stay tight and do not bleed into what surrounds them. The whole frame is in focus from the nearest person to the farthest corner of the ceiling: nothing is out of focus, nothing is soft, and there is no depth-of-field blur and no bokeh anywhere in the image.

CHANGE 3 - NOTHING IS PROJECTED ON THE FLOOR. The projected imagery lives on the WALLS and the CEILING only. The pale ash plank floor, the soft ivory wool rug, the oatmeal linen sectional sofa, the round natural-rattan ottoman, the tree and the gifts carry NO projected shapes at all - no snowflakes, no stars, no village, no Christmas figures, no gift village, no star, no sleigh, no falling light on them - they are lit only by the soft warm colour spill coming off the walls and sit in gentle shadow. Both children's faces and their cotton pyjamas catch that spill so both faces read clearly.

CHANGE 4 - the projector unit must NOT be visible. No projector, no side table, no stool, no glowing device anywhere in frame. The corner it would occupy is plain floor and the edge of the rug.

True-to-life colour and contrast exactly as they come straight out of the phone camera: no colour grading, no teal-and-orange grade, no vignette, no dramatic spotlighting, no heightened fantasy glow on the children, and no storybook-illustration or oil-painting finish on the projection. AND THE NIGHT SKY BETWEEN THE ELEMENTS IS DEEP AND DARK: everywhere the picture is not occupied by a lit element it is a rich near-black midnight sky of blackened indigo and very dark crimson, NOT white paint, NOT pale drywall and NOT a grey haze. It is that darkness between the elements that makes the bright ones burn.  HOW THE LIGHT SITS ON THE SURFACES, and this has to be exactly right. THE PROJECTION IS DENSE AND ROUGHLY NINETY PERCENT OPAQUE, and it is the brightest and most saturated thing in the frame. It lies on the room like an enormous backlit stained-glass window: deep, dense, fully saturated colour at full strength, crimson and ruby and warm gold and emerald at their richest, and it OVERPOWERS the colour of the surface underneath completely.  The material colour is overpowered by the projected colour; only surface texture and physical shadows remain legible through it. It is NOT pale, NOT pastel, NOT milky, NOT hazy, NOT semi-transparent, NOT a faint tint and NOT a washed-out overlay; there is no grey veil over any of it, and no part of the projection is so faint that the bare surface shows through as white.  AND IT WRAPS THE ARCHITECTURE. ONE SINGLE PROJECTOR sitting low in the near corner of the room throws all of this from that one point, so the entire picture keystones away from that origin and every surface takes it at its own angle. This is real projected light crossing a real three-dimensional room, so it obeys every surface it lands on.   The two board-lined ceiling planes and their central ridge remain clearly three-dimensional, meeting at a crisp ridge line. The sleigh and the ceiling picture fold at the ridge and are foreshortened along each slope, running straight across the faces of the three ash beams and every ceiling board, before continuing onto the walls. The tall concrete chimney breast receives a continuous picture with clear folds at its projecting corners. The pale ash slat panels, the ash window frames and mullions and the ash-framed opening carry the projection straight across their faces, with thin shadow breaks only at their actual edges. Bright imagery follows the built curves and planes, never a flat picture hovering across the room. All raised trim casts a real hard-edged shadow behind it. THE WOODEN FRAMES CARRY THE PICTURE TOO: the painted wooden window frames, sashes and glazing bars, the door and doorway casings, the skirting boards, the mantel and every other piece of wooden trim the beam crosses are covered by the same continuous projected imagery as the wall beside them - never left as bare white unlit outlines - with only a thin real shadow line at each edge. THIS IS THE KEY CORRECTION FOR THIS SET, AND THE PREVIOUS ATTEMPT FAILED IT: every exposed wooden beam, rafter, truss, purlin, window frame, mullion, muntin, glazing bar, door frame and casing is COVERED by the projection. Treat the projected image as one continuous sheet of coloured light that ignores the wood: where a beam crosses the ceiling, the stars, snowflakes, the sleigh, the reindeer and the golden trail continue straight over the beam's underside and both of its sides, cut into strips by its edges and shifted slightly by its depth, in the same full colour and brightness as the ceiling around it; where a window frame, mullion or door casing crosses the wall, the garlands, lanterns and scenery continue straight over its face in the same full colour as the wall beside it. Seen from the camera, the wooden members are camouflaged inside the picture - their shape reads only from thin shadow lines and a slight shift of the image along their edges, never from their own bare wood colour. NOT ONE beam, rafter, frame, mullion or casing appears as a plain bare wooden stripe, a dark silhouette or an unlit outline cutting through the picture. Only the window GLASS stays the dark night outside; the frames around the glass carry the picture. There is NO falloff anywhere. The picture stays strong, dense and fully coloured right into all four corners of the frame and right up into the corners of the ceiling, with no dim zone, no dark gap, no faded edge and no bare patch at any boundary of the room.  EVERY ELEMENT OF THE PROJECTION IS RAZOR SHARP: every elf, toy, ribbon, nutcracker detail, antler, snowflake, fir needle and window has precise edges. The thousands of points of light are DISCRETE PINPOINTS - tiny, tight, individually separated dots - and not soft round blobs, not fuzzy balls and not smeared streaks. There is NO bloom, NO glow halo, NO lens flare, NO atmospheric haze, NO soft-focus glaze and NO gaussian softness anywhere over the picture, and the brightest cores stay tight and do not bleed into what surrounds them. The whole frame is in focus from the nearest person to the farthest corner of the ceiling: nothing is out of focus, nothing is soft, and there is no depth-of-field blur and no bokeh anywhere in the image.

CHANGE 3 - NOTHING IS PROJECTED ON THE FLOOR. The projected imagery lives on the WALLS and the CEILING only. The pale ash plank floor, the soft ivory wool rug, the oatmeal linen sectional sofa, the round natural-rattan ottoman, the tree and the gifts carry NO projected shapes at all - no snowflakes, no stars, no village, no Christmas figures, no gift village, no star, no sleigh, no falling light on them - they are lit only by the soft warm colour spill coming off the walls and sit in gentle shadow. Both children's faces and their cotton pyjamas catch that spill so both faces read clearly.

CHANGE 4 - the projector unit must NOT be visible. No projector, no side table, no stool, no glowing device anywhere in frame. The corner it would occupy is plain floor and the edge of the rug.

NATURAL, LESS CINEMATIC LOOK - one small notch more natural than a polished film still: this is a candid photo a parent snapped on an iPhone at home, not a movie frame, a poster, a painting or an advert. True-to-life colour and contrast exactly as they come straight out of the phone camera: no colour grading, no teal-and-orange grade, no vignette, no dramatic spotlighting, no heightened fantasy glow on the children, and no storybook-illustration or oil-painting finish on the projection. The projection is still dense, bright and colourful, but its colour sits a touch closer to real life - rich, not neon - and it reads as the real light of a home Christmas projector falling on real walls: the plaster texture and wood grain show faintly through it, and the shadows keep a little more ordinary room detail, with the furniture, rug and floor clearly readable in the soft spill.

Vertical 9:16 photo, shot on an iPhone 16 Pro, natural handheld UGC look, extremely high quality, RAZOR SHARP EDGE TO EDGE AND CORNER TO CORNER at full 2K resolution with fine micro-detail legible everywhere - the fine cotton weave of the pyjamas, the grain of the floorboards, the tooth of the drywall, the grain of the pale ash ceiling boards and every individual pinpoint of the projection all crisply resolved - perfect lighting, perfectly exposed, hyper realistic, zero motion blur, zero depth-of-field blur, zero bokeh, zero bloom, zero glow haze, zero soft focus, nothing blurry, nothing smeared and nothing soft or gauzy anywhere in the frame, no CGI look. Normal wide-angle lens, straight vertical lines, no fisheye distortion.

Not a single bare, unlit, plain pale wood or white patch remains anywhere on the ceiling. Architectural occlusion shadows remain real and narrow. LOW ALONG THE WALLS, a SNOWY ICE-PALACE WINTER WONDERLAND: a glittering ice castle with glowing blue-and-gold windows and spiralling towers, skaters twirling on a frozen lake beneath strings of lanterns, polar bears and snowy owls, a white horse-drawn sleigh gliding across the ice, and tall frosted fir trees rising between them. Along the upper walls, strings of golden lights, red velvet bows and dense emerald garlands link the towers into a continuous Christmas panorama, with big soft snowflakes drifting and swirling down. ACROSS THE CEILING, Santa's red sleigh and eight reindeer fly in a wide arc past a big silver full moon, among twinkling golden stars, shooting stars and big glowing snowflakes, a long sparkling golden trail streaming behind them. Unmistakably Christmas Eve, glowing gold, ice blue and ruby red dominant with snow-white accents against a near-black midnight sky. One dense continuous panorama of projected light across the architecture, no isolated sticker icons, no carnival, no galaxy, no vortex. No projected shape floats physically in the room. AND THE NIGHT SKY BETWEEN THE ELEMENTS IS DEEP AND DARK: everywhere the picture is not occupied by a lit element it is a rich near-black midnight sky of blackened indigo and very dark crimson, NOT white paint, NOT pale drywall and NOT a grey haze. It is that darkness between the elements that makes the bright ones burn.  HOW THE LIGHT SITS ON THE SURFACES, and this has to be exactly right. THE PROJECTION IS DENSE AND ROUGHLY NINETY PERCENT OPAQUE, and it is the brightest and most saturated thing in the frame. It lies on the room like an enormous backlit stained-glass window: deep, dense, fully saturated colour at full strength, crimson and ruby and warm gold and emerald at their richest, and it OVERPOWERS the colour of the surface underneath completely.  The material colour is overpowered by the projected colour; only surface texture and physical shadows remain legible through it. It is NOT pale, NOT pastel, NOT milky, NOT hazy, NOT semi-transparent, NOT a faint tint and NOT a washed-out overlay; there is no grey veil over any of it, and no part of the projection is so faint that the bare surface shows through as white.  AND IT WRAPS THE ARCHITECTURE. ONE SINGLE PROJECTOR sitting low in the near corner of the room throws all of this from that one point, so the entire picture keystones away from that origin and every surface takes it at its own angle. This is real projected light crossing a real three-dimensional room, so it obeys every surface it lands on. The sleigh and the ceiling picture fold at the ridge and are foreshortened along each slope, running straight across the faces of the three ash beams and every ceiling board, before continuing onto the walls. The tall concrete chimney breast receives a continuous picture with clear folds at its projecting corners. The pale ash slat panels, the ash window frames and mullions and the ash-framed opening carry the projection straight across their faces, with thin shadow breaks only at their actual edges. Bright imagery follows the built curves and planes, never a flat picture hovering across the room. All raised trim casts a real hard-edged shadow behind it. THE WOODEN FRAMES CARRY THE PICTURE TOO: the painted wooden window frames, sashes and glazing bars, the door and doorway casings, the skirting boards, the mantel and every other piece of wooden trim the beam crosses are covered by the same continuous projected imagery as the wall beside them - never left as bare white unlit outlines - with only a thin real shadow line at each edge. THIS IS THE KEY CORRECTION FOR THIS SET, AND THE PREVIOUS ATTEMPT FAILED IT: every exposed wooden beam, rafter, truss, purlin, window frame, mullion, muntin, glazing bar, door frame and casing is COVERED by the projection. Seen from the camera, the wooden members are camouflaged inside the picture - their shape reads only from thin shadow lines and a slight shift of the image along their edges, never from their own bare wood colour. NOT ONE beam, rafter, frame, mullion or casing appears as a plain bare wooden stripe, a dark silhouette or an unlit outline cutting through the picture. Only the window GLASS stays the dark night outside; the frames around the glass carry the picture. There is NO falloff anywhere. The picture stays strong, dense and fully coloured right into all four corners of the frame and right up into the corners of the ceiling, with no dim zone, no dark gap, no faded edge and no bare patch at any boundary of the room.  EVERY ELEMENT OF THE PROJECTION IS RAZOR SHARP: every elf, toy, ribbon, nutcracker detail, antler, snowflake, fir needle and window has precise edges. The thousands of points of light are DISCRETE PINPOINTS - tiny, tight, individually separated dots - and not soft round blobs, not fuzzy balls and not smeared streaks. There is NO bloom, NO glow halo, NO lens flare, NO atmospheric haze, NO soft-focus glaze and NO gaussian softness anywhere over the picture, and the brightest cores stay tight and do not bleed into what surrounds them. 1.2-3.0s - THE HANDS lower THE PROJECTOR down through that gap toward the microcement in one continuous human descent, carrying a small natural tremor, easing slower and slower as the feet approach the microcement.
3.0-3.4s - the four feet of the stand touch the microcement; the hands relax their pressure and the object settles flat with a tiny real-world adjustment.
3.4-4.0s - THE HANDS open, release THE PROJECTOR and withdraw downward out of frame, leaving THE PROJECTOR standing alone on the counter.

[9] CAMERA AND OPTICS - one static POV looking down at the counter, vertical 9:16, normal wide-angle phone lens, very slight handheld drift only. No push-in, no orbit, no zoom, no tilt, no cut. Normal wide-angle only - no fisheye, no barrel distortion, no curved cabinet lines.

[10] LIGHTING - warm white under-cabinet light plus the soft warm glow of the small Christmas tree in the far corner, unchanged for the whole clip.

[11] PHYSICS - THE PROJECTOR is a solid rigid object with real weight and the hands are visibly carrying that weight. It stays essentially upright but is allowed the tiny tilts a real hand-held object has. The counter is hard: contact is a small firm settle. The fingertips whiten very slightly where they press the dome.

[12] AUDIO - DRY DOCUMENTARY AUDIO ONLY. The track contains exactly one thing and nothing else: a soft contact tap when the stand meets the microcement, in a quiet kitchen. Total silence apart from that. NO MUSIC, NO SONG, NO SCORE, NO SOUNDTRACK, NO INSTRUMENTS, NO VOICE.

[13] POSITIVE LOCKS - the movement reads as a real person's hands at every moment. The countertop and the kitchen behind it are visible in every single frame. The top face of THE PROJECTOR is BLACK. Exactly two hands. The descent travels the full 35 cm gap.
BANNED: any music, any song, any score, any soundtrack; robotic, mechanical, motorised, gliding, floating or animated movement; perfectly constant speed; the object appearing to hover unsupported; the countertop disappearing, the kitchen being replaced by a plain or dark background, an all-white product top, a lens on the front, a swivel camera head, a third hand, the product tipping over or spinning, the camera moving, a cut, subtitles, on-screen text.

### Video prompt
````text
ONE CONTINUOUS TAKE - ABSOLUTE RULE: this 5-second clip is a single uninterrupted real-time shot from the start image to the last frame. No cut, no jump cut, no hidden edit, no time skip, no dissolve, no zoom, no reframe. Everything in it happens continuously, frame to frame.

PROJECTED ANIMATION - THE MOST IMPORTANT THING IN THIS CLIP. The Christmas picture on the walls and the ash-lined gabled ceiling is a hand-drawn ANIMATED CHRISTMAS CARTOON playing at full speed, like a TV screen showing an animated Christmas movie - never a still picture. Only the projected picture is animated; the room and the children stay real photographic phone footage. Every drawn element below moves with big, bold, easy-to-read motion at normal playback speed, in EVERY frame from the first to the last, independent of the camera. Only the drawn buildings, trees and the moon stay put; everything else in the picture travels.
- SANTA'S SLEIGH AND EIGHT REINDEER: the WHOLE TEAM TRAVELS across the ceiling - it is never parked with only its legs moving. It flies in one smooth sweeping arc from where it is first seen across the ash-lined gabled ceiling toward the far side, covering about one quarter of the ceiling's width every second, so the sleigh is in a clearly different place every second and has crossed most of the ceiling by the end. The reindeer's legs gallop, heads bob, the sleigh rocks and dips, and the golden trail streams and curls behind it.
- CHIMNEY SMOKE - A HARD REQUIREMENT, NEVER SKIPPED: Every little snow-covered cottage along the frozen lake in the projected picture has a chimney on its roof, and from EVERY one of those chimneys a thick, bright white plume of smoke pours out continuously from the very first frame to the last - clearly visible against the dark night sky, never faint, never thin, never missing on any house. Each plume rises in a tall wavy column up the wall, swaying and curling, each puff climbing about one roof height every second, widening and fading higher up while fresh puffs keep pushing out of the chimney below. At every moment at least five separate smoke plumes are visibly rising across the walls. A house with a chimney but no rising smoke makes the clip wrong.
- WINTER WONDERLAND: the skaters glide and twirl across the frozen lake in wide loops, travelling about one lantern-width every second; the white horse-drawn sleigh glides steadily across the ice with the horse trotting; the polar bears walk slowly along the snowbanks; the snowy owls flap their wings and swoop between the towers; the ice castle's windows sparkle and the lanterns over the lake sway.
- SNOWFALL: big projected snowflakes drift steadily down across the whole picture, about a quarter of the wall height every second, turning as they fall - flat light on the wall and ceiling surfaces, never real snow in the room.
- SKY AND LIGHTS: the silver full moon glows; golden stars twinkle; now and then a shooting star streaks across the ceiling; bright gold sparkle waves travel along the garlands and ribbons.
PER-SECOND ANIMATION TRACK (5 s):
0-1s: the door opens onto the room; through the very first gap the sleigh is already flying and the skaters are already gliding and thick white smoke keeps pouring from every chimney.
1-2s: the sleigh team travels across the ash-lined gabled ceiling, clearly changing position, reindeer galloping; the skaters loop across the lake; the horse-drawn sleigh glides over the ice; the owls swoop; thick white smoke keeps pouring from every chimney; snow drifts down.
2-3s: the sleigh has flown on another quarter of the ceiling; the polar bears walk along the snowbanks; the lanterns sway; thick white smoke keeps pouring from every chimney.
3-4s: the sleigh keeps flying toward the far side of the ceiling; the skaters keep twirling; thick white smoke keeps pouring from every chimney; a shooting star streaks across.
4-5s: all of it still moving at the same pace in the final frame - sleigh flying, smoke pouring from every chimney, skaters gliding, owls swooping, snow swirling.
All of this is flat projected light lying on the real plaster, the ash glulam beams and ceiling boards, the ash slat panels and the ash window frames: it bends with the surfaces, never floats into the room, and nothing is projected on the floor, furniture or children.

CHILDREN - SPELLBOUND BY THE CEILING AND ALWAYS MOVING. EXACTLY TWO CHILDREN exist in this clip - THE BOY and THE GIRL described below - and nobody else ever appears: no third child, no extra kids in the background, on the sofa or in the doorway, no adult. The room reference photo is EMPTY, so there is no pose or spot to copy. When the first gap of the door opens, both children are ALREADY in the middle and far side of the room, at least three metres from the door - never standing in or near the doorway, never lined up facing the camera, never side by side. They were already watching the projection before the door opened and they are completely amazed by it: mouths wide open, eyebrows raised, gasping and laughing, eyes glued to the flying sleigh on the ceiling. They keep moving the whole time and follow the sleigh with their eyes, arms and feet. Per second:
0-1s: in the very first gap THE BOY is already walking slowly backwards across the rug, head tipped far back, one arm stretched straight up pointing at Santa's flying sleigh; THE GIRL, a few steps away from him, is up on tiptoe with both hands pressed to her cheeks, mouth open, staring straight up.
1-2s: THE BOY keeps stepping backwards and turns his body to follow the sleigh as it flies overhead, his pointing arm tracking it across the ceiling; THE GIRL runs three quick little steps across the rug in the same direction the sleigh is flying, one hand reaching up toward it.
2-3s: THE BOY looks over at THE GIRL, points up again and shouts with excitement, then looks straight back up; THE GIRL spins round once on the rug with her arms out and her face turned up to the ceiling, laughing.
3-4s: THE GIRL does two small excited bunny-hops on the spot, landing back on the rug at real speed, pointing up at the skaters twirling on the projected lake; THE BOY walks sideways past the ottoman with his head still tipped back, following the sleigh.
4-5s: both are still moving in the last frame - THE BOY still stepping and pointing up, THE GIRL trotting toward the Christmas tree while gazing up - never frozen.
They never stand still, never pose, never face the camera and never wait. Their feet stay on the floor: they never climb, jump onto or jump over the sofa, the ottoman or any furniture. Both stay in view: neither ducks behind furniture and pops up somewhere else. Real speed, real weight, grounded feet - no sliding, floating, slow motion or running in place. This child action never alters the camera movement.

CAMERA CONTINUITY: the entire five seconds are ONE slow, uninterrupted forward-and-upward glide. Forward travel and upward aim are coupled as one motion, already underway together in frame zero. Keep the same gentle pace and the same forward-to-upward ratio for every frame, including while the door opens and while crossing its sill. Travel only about 0.8 metres forward over the entire shot, with an even upward tilt of about 18 degrees over those same five seconds. The wreath, door-panel edges and later the back-wall details drift smoothly downward in the frame from the beginning while perspective advances very gently. There is no forward-only phase and no later tilt-only phase. Do not accelerate the beginning, hurry through the door, ease in, ease out, steepen the tilt, speed-ramp or catch up to an end composition. The final frame can remain near the threshold with the back wall, furniture and ceiling visible. The door opens naturally during the same slow glide; its opening is not a camera cue.

DIRECT ROOM ENTRY: the closed door in the start image is fitted directly into the great room's entrance wall and its far face opens straight into the furnished Christmas room shown in the room reference photo. ONE door, ONE thin normal threshold about 15 cm deep. The very first gap reveals the room itself, the already-moving children and the already-playing projection, close behind this door. No hall, foyer, vestibule, passage, empty approach space, second doorway or arch between this door and the room. The side doorway on the reference room's right wall is a lateral feature only and never lies ahead of the camera.

Wholesome family Christmas scene, children at home at Christmas.

TASK AND ASSETS: image-to-video, one continuous handheld phone POV take, vertical 9:16. The START IMAGE is frame 0.0s: a pale ash-wood flush door with fine vertical grooves, a matte oiled finish and a slim pale ash surrounding casing fully closed with a wreath, a woman's hand with very fair porcelain skin and a soft oatmeal-beige waffle-knit cotton sleeve on the brass knob. The ROOM REFERENCE IMAGE (attached as an image reference) controls only the room's architecture, furniture, colours and projected artwork; it contains no people and it is not an end frame, camera target or transition destination - never dissolve, cut, morph or snap into its composition. The two children come only from this text.

CONTINUOUS ACTION: during approximately the first 1.5 seconds the hand turns the knob and opens the door with a smooth, ordinary, unhurried push. The room behind it is already running: the projection is already playing and both children are already moving - the widening gap only uncovers more of a scene that was already happening. Nothing ignites or spreads as the door opens, and the door edge and frame stay plain wood - no glowing strip, no coloured fringe, no bright seam in the gap. The shot ends during the same gentle glide with the back wall, furniture and ceiling still visible.

CAMERA - eye height, normal wide-angle phone lens, a steady natural phone glide as described. No zoom, orbit, fisheye, lens warping, stop, second move or cut. The camera is the person's eyes; no phone, screen or device in frame. Hold the sharpness and exposure of the start image - crisp phone footage, in focus from the first frame to the last.

AUDIO - dry documentary sound only: the handle, the door swinging, the children gasping, laughing and calling out in delight, their footsteps on the floor, quiet room tone. (no BGM). No subtitles.

ROOM AND IDENTITY - a spacious double-height Japandi Christmas great room with a high GABLED CEILING lined in pale ash: two broad planes of slim pale ash boards rise to a crisp central ridge, crossed by three pale ash glulam beams. Warm white plaster walls with pale ash vertical slat panels on the lower walls. A tall chimney breast of smooth pale grey concrete stands on the back wall with a floating ash mantel; a wall of tall windows with slim pale ash frames and mullions occupies the left wall and a wide opening framed in pale ash sits on the right. A pale ash plank floor, a large low oatmeal linen sectional sofa, a round natural-rattan ottoman and a soft ivory wool rug.
THE BOY - a boy of about seven with fair skin and straight white-blond hair. THE GIRL - a girl of about four with fair skin, rosy cheeks and a short strawberry-blonde bob with a fringe. Both fully dressed in heather-grey and red brushed-cotton Christmas pyjama sets printed with little white snowflakes and red reindeer, with matching loose pyjama trousers and red wool socks.

PROJECTED SCENE CONTENT - LOW ALONG THE WALLS, a SNOWY ICE-PALACE WINTER WONDERLAND: a glittering ice castle with glowing blue-and-gold windows and spiralling towers, skaters twirling on a frozen lake beneath strings of lanterns, polar bears and snowy owls, a white horse-drawn sleigh gliding across the ice, and tall frosted fir trees rising between them. Along the upper walls, strings of golden lights, red velvet bows and dense emerald garlands link the towers into a continuous Christmas panorama, with big soft snowflakes drifting and swirling down. ACROSS THE CEILING, Santa's red sleigh and eight reindeer fly in a wide arc past a big silver full moon, among twinkling golden stars, shooting stars and big glowing snowflakes, a long sparkling golden trail streaming behind them. Unmistakably Christmas Eve, glowing gold, ice blue and ruby red dominant with snow-white accents against a near-black midnight sky. One dense continuous panorama of projected light across the architecture, no isolated sticker icons, no carnival, no galaxy, no vortex. No projected shape floats physically in the room.

BANNED - a third child or any extra people appearing at any moment; an adult; a child standing still, frozen, posed or waiting at any moment; the children standing in or next to the doorway when it opens; the children side by side or facing the camera; a child climbing onto or jumping on or over furniture; a child disappearing behind furniture and reappearing elsewhere; slow motion or floaty movement; a camera that moves, stops and moves again; a zoom; a cut; a glow along the door edge; light that grows or fills the room as the door opens; the projector unit visible; blur; music; subtitles.

MOTION GUARDRAILS - Failed if: the sleigh stays in one place or only its legs move; any chimney has no rising smoke; any house without rising smoke; the skaters, the horse-drawn sleigh or the polar bears do not travel; the owls do not fly; snow does not fall; the picture moves only because the camera moves; the animation starts late, pauses or freezes at the end; the whole picture slides as one sheet; any projected shape floats in the room.
````

- CHIMNEY SMOKE - A HARD REQUIREMENT, NEVER SKIPPED: Every little snow-covered cottage along the frozen lake in the projected picture has a chimney on its roof, and from EVERY one of those chimneys a thick, bright white plume of smoke pours out continuously from the very first frame to the last - clearly visible against the dark night sky, never faint, never thin, never missing on any house. Each plume rises in a tall wavy column up the wall, swaying and curling, each puff climbing about one roof height every second, widening and fading higher up while fresh puffs keep pushing out of the chimney below. At every moment at least five separate smoke plumes are visibly rising across the walls. A house with a chimney but no rising smoke makes the clip wrong.
- WINTER WONDERLAND: the skaters glide and twirl across the frozen lake in wide loops, travelling about one lantern-width every second; the white horse-drawn sleigh glides steadily across the ice with the horse trotting; the polar bears walk slowly along the snowbanks; the snowy owls flap their wings and swoop between the towers; the ice castle's windows sparkle and the lanterns over the lake sway.
- SNOWFALL: big projected snowflakes drift steadily down across the whole picture, about a quarter of the wall height every second, turning as they fall - flat light on the wall and ceiling surfaces, never real snow in the room.
- SKY AND LIGHTS: the silver full moon glows; golden stars twinkle; now and then a shooting star streaks across the ceiling; bright gold sparkle waves travel along the garlands and ribbons.
PER-SECOND ANIMATION TRACK (while the camera tilts down):
0-1s: snow streams down the whole projection; the sleigh is already flying across the ceiling; the skaters are already gliding and thick white smoke keeps pouring from every chimney.
1-2s: the sleigh has flown a quarter of the way across the ceiling; the skaters loop across the lake; the horse-drawn sleigh glides over the ice; the owls swoop; thick white smoke keeps pouring from every chimney.
2-3s: the sleigh flies on across the ceiling; the polar bears walk along the snowbanks; the lanterns sway; thick white smoke keeps pouring from every chimney.
3-4s: the sleigh keeps flying toward the far side of the ceiling; the skaters keep twirling; thick white smoke keeps pouring from every chimney; a shooting star streaks across.
4-5s: sleigh flying, smoke pouring from every chimney, skaters gliding, owls swooping, snow swirling - all still moving in the last frame.
All of this is flat projected light lying on the real plaster, the pale ash trim, the ash slat panelling and the window frames: it bends with the surfaces, never floats into the room, and nothing is projected on the floor, the bed or the boy.

CAMERA - ONE slow, small tilt DOWN, the opposite of the other room clips in this set.
0.0s: the clip starts EXACTLY on the start image - the same framing, with the fully projected ceiling at the top of the frame. THE BOY BOUNCES LIKE A REAL CHILD ON A REAL MATTRESS - natural, believable and unexaggerated, at real speed for all five seconds. Small, springy, continuous bounces on the same spot in the middle of the bed: his feet leave the mattress by only about 10 to 20 centimetres, about two bounces per second, the soft mattress visibly sinking under his feet on every landing and pushing him back up. REAL-TIME SPEED, NOT SLOW MOTION: each bounce is quick and snappy, taking only about half a second from push-off to landing under normal gravity, exactly like a real child filmed at normal speed - the footage is never slowed down, never time-stretched and never dreamy. Preserve the exact child identity, clothing, furniture, colours and the full projected artwork of the start image; its framing is the FIRST frame of this clip.

SUBJECTS:
THE BOY - exactly one child, a boy of about seven with fair skin and straight white-blond hair, fully dressed for winter in a long-sleeved heather-grey and red brushed-cotton Christmas top printed with little white snowflakes and red reindeer, with matching loose lounge trousers and red wool socks, standing on the bed. No second child, no adult, no pet.
THE PROJECTOR - a single Christmas projector switched on and running on the bedroom floor, out of shot. One lens throws one wide cone of light across the walls and the whole tray ceiling. Everything glowing on the plaster and the wood is that one cone landing.
THE PICTURE - the animated scene is the exact Christmas panorama visible in the start image. LOW ALONG THE WALLS, a SNOWY ICE-PALACE WINTER WONDERLAND: a glittering ice castle with glowing blue-and-gold windows and spiralling towers, skaters twirling on a frozen lake beneath strings of lanterns, polar bears and snowy owls, a white horse-drawn sleigh gliding across the ice, and tall frosted fir trees rising between them. Along the upper walls, strings of golden lights, red velvet bows and dense emerald garlands link the towers into a continuous Christmas panorama, with big soft snowflakes drifting and swirling down. ACROSS THE CEILING, Santa's red sleigh and eight reindeer fly in a wide arc past a big silver full moon, among twinkling golden stars, shooting stars and big glowing snowflakes, a long sparkling golden trail streaming behind them. Unmistakably Christmas Eve, glowing gold, ice blue and ruby red dominant with snow-white accents against a near-black midnight sky. One dense continuous panorama of projected light across the architecture, no isolated sticker icons, no carnival, no galaxy, no vortex. No projected shape floats physically in the room.

AUDIO: room tone, mattress springs compressing on each landing, bedding rustling, a boy laughing and gasping in delight. (no BGM). No subtitles.

````text
Extremely high quality candid vertical iPhone photo taken from a hallway looking into a family's living room on a winter evening: a young mother kneels on the bare floor with a huge tangled knot of old multicoloured Christmas string lights in front of her, her arm stretched out to push the plug into a wall socket, while her two small children stand behind her watching the Christmas tree.

Use the FIRST attached reference photograph for BOTH the staging AND the camera position - the mother kneeling in the near foreground, the knot of lights on the floor beside her, her arm reaching out to the low wall socket, the two small children standing behind her, the tall Christmas tree on the right and the plain white wall corner the camera is looking past. COPY ITS CAMERA POSITION AND ITS COMPOSITION EXACTLY. Copy nothing else from it: the room, the mother and the children are all described below and are all different people in a different house.

FRAMING AND ANGLE - COPY THE CAMERA FROM THE REFERENCE: the camera is handheld at about the chest height of a standing adult, tilted very slightly down, standing in the next room and looking in through a doorway, so a plain white wall corner with a white skirting board runs floor to ceiling down the RIGHT EDGE of the frame and takes up roughly the right eighth of the picture - we are looking past it into the living room. The living room floor fills the bottom half of the frame and runs away into depth. The mother kneels in the lower LEFT of the frame, seen from behind at three quarters, close to the camera and large in the picture: her head and shoulders sit around the middle of the frame and the bottom edge of the picture cuts her just below the knees. The knot of lights lies on the bare floor immediately to her right, between her and that white wall corner, around the centre of the lower third of the picture. The Christmas tree stands on the RIGHT of the frame just inside the white corner, tall enough that the top edge of the picture cuts off its top. The two children stand on the floor in the middle distance between the mother and the tree, small in the frame, their heads in the middle third of the picture. Nobody is looking at the camera and nobody is posing. This is NOT a side angle, NOT a low floor-level angle and NOT a symmetrical composition.

THE KNOT AND THE PLUG: on the bare floorboards immediately to the mother's right lies a big ugly knot of old-fashioned Christmas string lights - dark green flex wound and knotted into a messy nest about the width of two floorboards across, studded with fat vintage multicoloured bulbs in red, green, blue and yellow, with a white in-line plug body and loops of slack wire lifting out of the top of it. She holds the end of that flex in her outstretched right hand and the two-pin plug is an inch from a white electrical socket set low on the white wall corner at the right of the frame. It is not plugged in yet and every bulb in the knot is dark.

THE CONNECTION - THIS IS THE IMPORTANT PART: one single continuous run of that SAME dark green flex with the SAME fat multicoloured bulbs leaves the far side of the knot, travels away across the floorboards to the right, passes behind the children, reaches the foot of the Christmas tree and climbs up into its lower branches, where it is plainly the very same string that is wound round and round the whole tree above. Same wire colour, same bulb size, same bulb spacing, one unbroken line. The eye must be able to follow the wire from the plug in her hand, through the knot on the floor, across the floor and up onto the tree without a single break or gap. The heap on the floor is NOT a separate loose pile with nothing leading out of it, and the tree is NOT wired to anything else.

THE TREE IS NOT SWITCHED ON: because the plug is still in her hand, every bulb on the tree is dark and unlit. The tree is not glowing, no light comes off it, and it throws no coloured light onto the floor, the walls or the children. It is still fully decorated and still reads as a beautiful Christmas tree - the baubles catch the warm lamplight in the room - it is simply not lit yet.

THE ROOM: a calm Japandi living room. Walls of smooth warm white plaster above a low band of pale ash panelling with white skirting, wide pale white-ash floorboards running away from the camera, a low oatmeal linen sofa along the left wall with a deep red wool blanket folded over its back, a soft cream flat-woven wool rug on the floor in front of it, a low pale ash side table behind the sofa carrying a round white paper-lantern table lamp that is switched on and glowing, and a tall rectangular window with a slim pale ash frame on the back wall showing a dark blue winter evening and snow on the sill outside. Preserve the plain white wall corner at the RIGHT EDGE exactly as in the FIRST reference.

THE TREE: a big real fir, dense and dark green, dressed with matte red and white ceramic baubles, small natural-wood bead garlands, dried orange slices, a wide cream linen ribbon wound down it and a gold star on top, with a heavy cream cable-knit tree skirt gathered round its base and four presents wrapped in brown kraft paper and red ribbon sitting on it.

THE MOTHER: about 31, very fair skin and long straight platinum-blonde hair tied in a loose low ponytail, an oversized deep red chunky cable-knit sweater with the sleeves pushed up her forearms, charcoal grey joggers and thick oatmeal socks, no shoes. She kneels sitting back on her heels, her left hand holding a clearly visible palm-sized roll of beige masking tape with an open cardboard core just above her left thigh, her right arm stretched right out towards the socket with the plug pinched between her thumb and fingers, her head turned in profile watching the plug. She is concentrating, slightly fed up, not smiling.

THE CHILDREN: a boy of about six standing on the rug behind his mother, messy light-brown hair and a rosy freckled face, navy-and-white striped pyjamas, barefoot, his mouth wide open in a huge exaggerated gasp of amazement and both hands up near his cheeks, staring at the tree. Beside him a girl of about four with pale blonde wavy hair held back by a small cream bow, a sage-green flannel nightgown printed with tiny white stars, crouched down on one knee with her right arm flung out pointing straight at the tree. Neither child blocks the mother, the knot of lights or the tree.

COLOUR: warm domestic evening white balance, the way an iPhone renders a lamp-lit living room - warm but not orange. The picture is NOT sepia, NOT amber-washed and NOT heavily graded. Skin reads natural, the white wall corner reads clean white, the floorboards read as real wood and the sweater reads as true deep red.

LIGHTING AND QUALITY: the room is lit by the warm table lamp plus soft ambient light - cosy and warm, but still bright enough that every face, every twist of wire in the knot and the whole tree are clearly readable, with no dark corners where detail disappears and no blown highlights. Shot on an iPhone 16 Pro and straight out of the camera roll - an ordinary everyday snapshot somebody took of their family, not a magazine photo and not a rendered image. Extremely high quality, tack sharp and in focus everywhere from the mother in the foreground to the tree behind her, crisp detail in every individual bulb and every twist of flex in the tangle, realistic skin, hair and knitwear texture. Absolutely nothing blurry: no motion blur, no soft focus, no background blur, no bokeh, no grain, no film look, no cinematic grade, no vignette. Normal wide-angle lens, vertical lines vertical and parallel, no fisheye distortion.

FRAMING: leave the upper LEFT of the image - roughly the top eighth of the frame on the wall side - as plain uncluttered wall, free of pictures, shelves, garland, hanging decorations and lettering, so a caption can sit there. Do not pull the camera back or shrink the mother to make that room.

No captions, no watermarks, no added lettering anywhere in the image, no writing on any clothing, no user-interface elements, no mute or speaker icons and no social-media overlays.

THE TAPE ROLL: one real roll of beige masking tape, clearly recognizable as a thick circular tape band with a hollow cardboard centre, held visibly in her LEFT hand just above her left thigh. Her RIGHT hand keeps the plug and reaches toward the socket exactly as in the FIRST reference. Preserve the mother's kneeling body position, camera crop and scale; only the left-hand grip changes to hold the tape. The tape is not floating and is not on the floor. No tape dispenser.

REFERENCE ROLES: @Image 1 owns the exact camera, framing, scale, mother position, tree position, wire continuity and both children's poses; the mother and both children are new people, as described above. @Image 2 supplies only the Japandi material palette of smooth warm white plaster, pale ash, oatmeal linen and deep red. Ignore its children, clothing, composition, projected imagery and darkness. The new room is clearly lit by its lamp and ambient fill as described above, with plain walls and no projector patterns. ````text
Single continuous handheld iPhone shot, 5 seconds, vertical 9:16, hyperrealistic. No cuts, no zoom, no slow motion, no speed ramp. The camera itself IS the phone being held by a man standing in the hallway doorway, so we see ONLY what he sees. No phone, no screen, no hand, no fingers, no thumb, no arm, no sleeve, no device and no object ever appears in the foreground or along any edge of the frame.

Start frame: a platinum-blonde mother in a deep red cable-knit sweater kneels on pale ash floorboards in the lower left, her right arm stretched out to a white wall socket on the white corner at the right of the frame, holding the plug of a big tangled knot of unlit multicoloured Christmas string lights that lies on the floor beside her. One continuous run of that same string leads away across the floor and up into the decorated but unlit Christmas tree standing on the right. A boy in navy-and-white striped pyjamas and a small girl in a sage-green nightgown stand behind her near the oatmeal linen sofa.

ACTION TIMELINE:
0.0-0.8s - The mother pushes the plug the last few centimetres into the socket. Her hand moves slowly and steadily, her head still turned in profile watching it. Everything else in the room is calm and still. The tree is dark. The camera is locked where it stands in the start frame, only natural handheld sway.

AT 0.8s - ONE SINGLE INSTANT, AND EVERYTHING IN IT HAPPENS ON THE SAME FRAME. The two pins seat in the socket; every bulb on the knot and on the tree flashes on for a split second; and in that same instant a burst of orange flame erupts low in the Christmas tree where the string meets the branches and the tree catches fire. On that exact same frame the man holding the camera shouts "Oh, shoot" and the whole frame jolts as he flinches backwards. The fire is not delayed, it does not build up, it does not start small and grow for a second first - the tree is already burning on the same frame the plug goes in.

0.8-2.0s - The flames climb the tree fast and realistically, running up through the dry branches, orange and yellow, throwing real flickering firelight across the warm white plaster walls, the floorboards, the sofa and the three faces, with dark smoke rolling up off the top of the tree toward the ceiling. Ornaments glow and the branches blacken. The mother recoils hard away from the socket, twists round onto her hands and pushes herself up off the floor. The boy and the girl scream and turn toward the camera. The man with the camera takes ONLY TWO OR THREE SHORT STEPS BACKWARDS and then stops and stands his ground. That small retreat is finished by about 1.6s. He does not walk backwards down the hallway, he does not run, he does not retreat any further at any later point in the clip.

2.0-5.0s - The camera is now STANDING STILL in the hallway just behind the doorway, holding its position with only natural handheld sway, not moving backwards at all. The mother is on her feet and runs straight toward the camera with her arms out, scooping the two children ahead of her, all three sprinting toward the lens and growing rapidly larger in the frame, faces wide with panic, the boy's arms pumping, the little girl half carried. Because the camera is no longer backing away, they close the distance fast and come right up to the lens. End frame: the three of them very close and very large in the frame mid-run, almost filling it, the burning tree bright behind them through the doorway.

AUDIO: natural room sound only. One male voice, off camera behind the lens, shouting "Oh, shoot" exactly on the frame the tree catches fire - nothing else from him before it, nothing else after it. Then the crackle and roar of the fire, the mother shouting wordlessly, the two children screaming, bare feet running on wood. NO music, no score, no background song, no narration, no added sound effects beyond these.

REALISM - HARD RULES: the mother, the boy and the girl keep exactly the same faces, hair, clothes and body shapes as the start frame throughout. The room stays the same room - same warm white plaster walls and pale ash panelling band, same oatmeal linen sofa with the deep red blanket, same pale ash floorboards, same rug, same lamp, same window. The fire behaves like real fire on a real dry conifer: it spreads along branches, it does not float, it does not appear as a glowing overlay, it casts real moving light and real shadows on every surface, and it produces real dark smoke. Real phone-camera look with slight sensor noise and natural motion blur only from the handheld movement. No text overlays, no captions, no watermark, no on-screen graphics.

POSITIVE LOCKS: one continuous take from 0.0s to 5.0s with no cut anywhere. Everything plays at real-time speed. The camera never zooms. The ONLY camera movement in the entire clip is a short backwards flinch of two or three steps between 0.8s and 1.6s; before 0.8s the camera holds its start-frame position and after 1.6s the camera holds its new position and stays there until the end. BANNED: the camera walking or running backwards down the hallway, the camera retreating for more than three steps, the camera moving away from the family during their run, the family staying the same size in the frame as they run. Nobody stops moving once the fire starts.

PROP CONTINUITY: she begins with the existing roll of beige masking tape in her left hand; it stays gripped in that hand as she rises and runs. The tape never becomes a second plug or vanishes. The right hand alone seats the electrical plug.

ACROSS THE CEILING, Santa's red sleigh and eight reindeer fly in a wide arc past a big silver full moon, among twinkling golden stars, shooting stars and small flat drawn snowflakes, a long sparkling golden trail streaming behind them. Unmistakably Christmas Eve, glowing gold, ice blue and ruby red dominant with snow-white accents against a near-black midnight sky. One dense continuous panorama of projected light across the architecture, no isolated sticker icons, no carnival, no galaxy, no vortex. No projected shape floats physically in the room.

AUDIO: room tone, mattress springs compressing on each landing, bedding rustling, a boy laughing and gasping in delight. (no BGM). No subtitles.

DO NOT INCLUDE: a still, frozen or printed-looking projection; a projection that only twinkles; a sleigh parked in one place; chimneys without smoke; a house without rising smoke; skaters, a horse-drawn sleigh or polar bears that do not travel; a realistic or three-dimensional owl or bird; drawn snow on the walls that does not move; any snowflake, sparkle or particle in the air of the room, in front of the boy or in front of the camera, or anything that looks like real snow falling inside the room; bare, white or unprojected ceiling or wall; a camera that starts higher, wider or anywhere other than the start image; a static, locked-off camera; a tilt up; a pan, zoom, push-in or pull-back; any drawn animal or figure that looks real or leaves the wall; bare or unprojected ceiling in the first frame; any house or cottage without a thick rising smoke column; fewer than twelve things moving at once; a calm or slow projection; slow-motion or floaty jumping; high exaggerated leaps; the boy changing size or shape; real snow or particles in the air of the room; any music; the projector unit in shot; a second child; an adult; blur; a cut; on-screen text; subtitles.
